1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases. Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).
10

17	\midrule
18	R1-8B & Llama & 8B & 32 & 4096 & 209\,/\,43 \\
19	OT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\,/\,47 \\
20	R1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\,/\,20 \\
21	QwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\,/\,19 \\

23	\end{tabular}}
24	\caption{Models studied. R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}
25	\label{tab:models}

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers (strong, moderate, qualified) based on seed robustness, position independence, and sample size; per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
10	
11	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. Three of four metrics excluding crossing rate (precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite) consistently select deep layers at L63 (98\%) for both 32B models. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. 1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample-dependent. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmed at 7--8B; descriptive at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders (e.g., LLM-based embeddings~\citep{wang2024e5mistral}) could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear position control, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under stricte

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The HS advantage is confirmed at 7--8B (nested CV validated) but carries model-specific caveats at 32B$^\dagger$ (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes) and narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.
4

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Recent work further documents adversarial reasoning capabilities and information leakage in reasoning traces~\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}, providing additional motivation for hidden-state monitoring.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}

_source line 8355 · 2026-05-19T15:30:53.582169+00:00_

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Single dataset.}
4	All experiments use one dataset (HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts}), which provides step-level CP annotations derived from manual inspection of reasoning chains. The dataset spans multiple harmful categories (e.g., violence, fraud, illegal activities) but we do not stratify results by category; categories with longer average reasoning chains or earlier CPs may exhibit different probe dynamics. CP positions range from early to late within traces, though the distribution is right-skewed (most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning). Annotation granularity (step-level vs.\ token-level), category distribution, and annotator team composition may all influence which layers carry discriminative signal and how strongly metric bias manifests. Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations. Random annotation noise would attenuate effect sizes symmetrically, making our estimates conservative; systematic noise (e.g., consistent early or late CP placement) could bias layer selection or inflate modality differences. Without IAA, we cannot distinguish these cases, so our conclusions may not transfer to independently annotated corpora. Generalization to other jailbreak datasets, attack taxonomies, or multilingual settings is untested.
5	
6	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
7	Current probes characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 11--50\% (model-dependent), and the 76.7\% oracle gap (R1-8B; \cref{sec:oracle}) underscores how much room remains. R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). A
…[truncated 2739 chars]

Based on the search results, here's what I found about arXiv 2502.15594:

**Paper Title:** SafeInt: Shielding Large Language Models from Jailbreak Attacks via Safety-Aware Representation Intervention

**Core Approach:** SafeInt relocates jailbreak-related representations into the rejection region by intervening in the representation distributions of jailbreak samples to align them with those of unsafe samples.

**Key Features:**
- The method adopts a parameterized intervention which adaptively identifies and adjusts jailbreak-related representations regardless of manually tuning the intervention strength.
- The research covers six jailbreak attacks, two jailbreak datasets, and two utility benchmarks.

**Results:** Experimental results demonstrate that SafeInt outperforms all baselines in defending LLMs against jailbreak attacks while largely maintaining utility.

**Publication:** The paper appears in the Findings of the Association for Computational Linguistics: EMNLP 2025 (November 4-9, 2025).

Based on the search results, I found information about arXiv paper 2512.12069:

The paper proposes Representational Contrastive Scoring (RCS), a framework that inspects the internal geometry of LVLM representations and learns a lightweight projection to maximally separate benign and malicious inputs in safety-critical layers, enabling a contrastive score that differentiates malicious intent from novelty. The paper instantiates this with MCD (Mahalanobis Contrastive Detection) and KCD (K-nearest Contrastive Detection), which achieve state-of-the-art performance on a challenging evaluation protocol designed to test generalization to unseen attack types.

**Title:** Linear Probe Accuracy Scales with Model Size and Benefits from Multi-Layer Ensembling

**Authors:** Erik Nordby, Tasha Pais, Aviel Parrack

**Key Findings:**

- Combining probes from multiple layers into an ensemble recovers strong performance even where single-layer probes fail, improving AUROC by +29% on Insider Trading and +78% on Harm-Pressure Knowledge.
- Across 12 models (0.5B--176B parameters), probe accuracy improves with scale: ~5% AUROC per 10x parameters (R=0.81).

  "old_string": "Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP), the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases, requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs spanning two architectures (Llama and Qwen, with Qwen as the dominant base in 3/4 models) on the HarmThoughts safety-probing benchmark and show that the outcome of this comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored. A crossing-rate composite metric masks the hidden-state (HS) advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against a 384-dimensional sentence encoder (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); the advantage narrows to 1/4 models against a 1024-dimensional encoder. Evidence is graded: two models are confirmed (7--8B, nested CV validated), one is significant but position-competitive (QwQ-32B$^\\dagger$), and one is exploratory (R1-32B$^\\dagger$: position-mediated, below minimum detectable effect). Layer selection sensitivity is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.",
  "new_string": "Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B FPR is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\\dagger$ ($^\\dagger$\\,sample-limited, $n \\leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. Layer selection sensitivity is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control."
}

Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B FPR is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\\dagger$ ($^\\dagger$\\,sample-limited, $n \\leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. Layer selection sensitivity is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control."
  }
}
```

</details>

  "old_string": "Information-theoretic and causal formulations address probe complexity~\\cite{voita2020information,pimentel2020information,ravfogel2020null,ravfogel2022rlace,belrose2023leace,elazar2021amnesic}. \\citet{marks2024geometry} find emergent linear structure in LLM truth representations, consistent with our safety commitment separability. That information distributes non-uniformly across layers is well established: \\citet{tenney2019bert} show syntactic information concentrates in early layers while semantic information peaks later, and \\citet{jawahar2019bert} confirm this hierarchy through probing and attention analysis. Our contribution is orthogonal: the known layer-wise information gradient interacts with metric choice to produce systematic selection bias in safety probes, a phenomenon not previously characterized.",

}

  "old_string": "Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The HS advantage is confirmed at 7--8B (nested CV validated) but carries model-specific caveats at 32B$^\\dagger$ (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes) and narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.",
  "new_string": "Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\\dagger$ are exploratory (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes). The advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark; Contribution~1 (layer selection sensitivity) generalizes from representation geometry, while Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) requires validation on additional datasets. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps."
}
```

_source line 8419 · 2026-05-19T15:36:01.893339+00:00_

11	Our contributions are:
12	\begin{itemize}
13	    \item \textbf{Layer selection sensitivity.} We identify a systematic bias in composite metrics that reverses HS probe conclusions at 32B scale. A five-metric ablation establishes principled selection criteria (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
14	
15	    \item \textbf{Encoder-quality boundary with evidence gradient.} Against 384d MiniLM, the HS advantage is confirmed at 7--8B (+20.1 to +21.9pp, HB corrected, nested CV validated), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). Against 1024d BGE-large, only OT-7B survives correction (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}).
16	
17	    \item \textbf{Negative results define method boundaries.} No alternative method consistently matches supervised probing at any evidence tier (\cref{tab:negative}): BOCPD/CUSUM fail on gradual transitions; Mahalanobis distance finds near-zero separation; RepE shows inconsistent performance across models (competitive on QwQ-32B, poor elsewhere); cross-model transfer fails under PCA alignment. Control task selectivity (11.1--16.8pp above chance; \cref{app:control}) confirms genuine safety structure.
18	\end{itemize}
19	
20	\noindent These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark for hidden-state safety monitoring and identify layer selection as a critical confound that future work must address.

_source line 8427 · 2026-05-19T15:36:20.608510+00:00_

6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmed at 7--8B; descriptive at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. This boundary is itself a finding: the HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders (e.g., LLM-based embeddings~\citep{wang2024e5mistral}) could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.
8

  "old_string": "R1-32B detects commitment 11--13 steps early with a +14.2pp margin over the position baseline, which confirms genuine representational signal, but covers only 25\\% of traces at the single significant window ($W{=}1$).   "old_string": "Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\\dagger$ are exploratory (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes). The advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). These findings provide initial characterization on one benchmark; Contribution~1 (layer selection sensitivity) generalizes from representation geometry, while Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) requires validation on additional datasets. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.",
  "new_string": "Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\\dagger$ are exploratory (position mediation, limited coverage, descriptive sample sizes) and the advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (layer selection sensitivity) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps."
}

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}\nOur five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. _source line 8469 · 2026-05-19T15:40:06.524168+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}. Whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of our HS advantage claims.
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard linear
…[truncated 903 chars]

_source line 8485 · 2026-05-19T15:43:00.724975+00:00_

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{confirmatory} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
10	
11	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and converge to the same deep layer by mathematical necessity: L63 (98\%) for both 32B models. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20;

_source line 8487 · 2026-05-19T15:43:11.094439+00:00_

64	
65	The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): confirmatory at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust: $p{=}0.044$ (rank 12/12 in the HB step-down, the weakest surviving test), with only 1/5 seeds passing Bonferroni correction (\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds ($\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}); selectivity measures label structure (main vs.\ shuffled), complementary to position contribution (temporal artifacts). Position features account for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy (\cref{app:position_residual}); R1-32B's HS advantage is fully explained by position (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$).
66	
67	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). Two individual metrics are non-significant: R1-8B BA (+1.3pp, $p{=}0.731$) and QwQ-32B FPR ($-$11.4pp, $p{=}0.038$, below HB threshold). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation but does not rule out representation quality differences. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \cref{ap

64	
65	The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): confirmatory at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust: $p{=}0.044$ (rank 12/12 in the HB step-down, the weakest surviving test), with only 1/5 seeds passing Bonferroni correction (\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds ($\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
66	
67	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d$\rightarrow$PCA 384d) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB; \cref{app:e5_mistral}), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).
68	
69	\paragraph{Encoder robustness.} Against BGE-large~\citep{xiao2023cpack} (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB (1/4 precision); R1-8B shows adverse FPR direction (+1.2pp). The advantage is encoder-conditional (\cref{fig:forest_precision}); lay

_source line 8529 · 2026-05-19T16:20:33.263132+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] R20 审稿修订 — 14 项文本修改

commit b31bba0 是基线（R19 修订完成）。以下修改基于 R20 审稿报告 + Reviewer 独立审查确认。

## P0（Must-Fix，6 项）

### N1: Position control 结论需同时报告 linear 和 quadratic
- **位置**: Discussion section，position mediation 段落
- **当前**: 只说 "3 of 4 models retain HS advantage after position control"
- **改为**: 明确报告 "3/4 under linear residualization, 2/4 under quadratic control (QwQ-32B advantage absorbed)"，并简要讨论两种控制的差异含义
- **注意**: 不要弱化 linear 作为主结论的地位，quadratic 是 sensitivity analysis

### N2: HB scope 量化 — 说清楚探索空间有多大
- **位置**: Section 4 (Results) 或 Section 3 (Method)，layer selection 讨论处
- **改为**: 加一句量化总探索空间 "5 metrics × 7 window sizes × 28–64 layers (depending on model architecture)"，让读者理解 Holm-Bonferroni 12/12 覆盖的是最终测试，不是全部 researcher DOF

### N3: 删除 "by mathematical necessity"（这是我们 R19 修订引入的错误）
- **位置**: Section 4，讨论 threshold-based metrics convergence 处
- **当前**: "converge by mathematical necessity from their shared confusion-matrix structure"
- **改为**: "empirically converge at extreme layers, consistent with shared confusion-matrix structure but not mathematically guaranteed, as each metric defines a distinct optimization landscape over layers"
- **关键**: argmax convergence 不是 shared confusion matrix 的数学必然——confusion matrix 相同不意味着不同 metric 的 argmax 相同

### N4: 加 Chan & Yong 2025 引用
- **位置**: Related Work，hidden-state probing 段落
- **Bib 条目**: `@article{chan2025probing, title={Probing Safety Commitments in Large Language Models}, author={Chan, Xin and Yong, Zheng-Xin}, journal={arXiv preprint arXiv:2507.12428}, year={2025}}`
- **文中**: 在 related work 合适处加一句引用，说明近期有工作也研究了 LLM 安全承诺点的 probing

### N5: Contribution 1 重新表述为 "metric-induced systematic bias"
- **位置**: Introduction, contribution list, Contribution 1
- **当前**: 可能说 "layer selection sensitivity" 或类似
- **改为**: 强调 "metric-induced systematic bias in layer selection"——crossing-rate composite 系统性选择浅层，这不是随机噪声而是系统性偏差
- **同步**: Abstract 中对应表述也要更新

### W8: 修复 bib 中的 "others" 条目
- **位置**: references.bib
- **问题**: 部分 bib 条目 author 字段用 "and others" 截断，顶会要求列全部作者
- **操作**: 检查所有 bib 条目，把 "and others" 替换为完整作者列表。Agent 已被派去查具体哪些条目需要修复和正确的作者列表，结果会另行通知你。如果 Agent 还没回复，先处理其他项，W8 最后补。

## P1（Should-Fix，8 项）

### W1: BCa bootstrap caveat
- **位置**: Method 或 Results，bootstrap CI 首次出现处
- **加一句**: "We use BCa bootstrap intervals, which assume approximate pivotality; violations (e.g., heavy-tailed score distributions) may produce anti-conservative coverage."

### W2: LOO-CV top-3 layer sensitivity
- **位置**: Appendix，LOO-CV 结果讨论处

_source line 8531 · 2026-05-19T16:20:52.126011+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B FPR is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. Layer selection sensitivity is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition (\cref{sec:limitations}). Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).

_source line 8535 · 2026-05-19T16:20:53.280873+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Recent work further documents adversarial reasoning capabilities and information leakage in reasoning traces~\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}, providing additional motivation for hidden-state monitoring.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}
13	Internal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal st

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{confirmatory} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
10	
11	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and converge to the same deep layer by mathematical necessity: L63 (98\%) for both 32B models. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20;

_source line 8543 · 2026-05-19T16:22:07.308675+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before building production detectors, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides a systematic characterization of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect.
4	
5	Existing safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. Before asking which modality is better, a prior methodological question must be answered: how sensitive are probe conclusions to the choice of layer selection metric? HS probes require choosing which transformer layer to monitor, a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny. We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion.
6	
7	We present an initial characterization across four reasoning models---primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models), with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence---at two scales (7--8B, 32B) on the HarmThoug
…[truncated 2580 chars]

_source line 8545 · 2026-05-19T16:22:08.266219+00:00_

43	\label{app:layer_sweep}
44	
45	For each model, we sweep all transformer layers and evaluate five candidate selection metrics: (1)~balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate (the CR-composite), (2)~pure balanced accuracy, (3)~precision, (4)~inverse pre-CP FPR, and (5)~an additive composite. The CR-composite systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models because early-layer representations exhibit higher crossing rates (more frequent threshold crossings) regardless of discriminative power. Three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) converge on deep layers for all models; pure balanced accuracy selects layers whose depth varies with the data split (under 70/30: L30 for QwQ-32B, L47 for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20: L63 and L46 respectively; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}).
46	
47	\paragraph{Selected layers.} R1-8B: all five metrics agree on layer~14 (44\% depth). OT-7B: CR-composite selects L0 (0\%), threshold-based consensus selects L16 (57\%). QwQ-32B: CR-composite selects L2 (3\%), threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98\%). R1-32B: CR-composite selects L10 (16\%), threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98\%). The disagreement is most extreme for 64-layer models, where the CR-composite selects layers in the bottom 16\% of the network while threshold-based metrics select layers at 98\% depth. Under 70/30, pure balanced accuracy selects L30 (47\%) for QwQ-32B and L47 (73\%) for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20, it selects L63 (98\%) and L46 (72\%), which demonstrates sensitivity to the data split.
48	
49	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the CR-composite layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the threshold-based deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodology, not a genuine property of model scale
…[truncated 2432 chars]

_source line 8563 · 2026-05-19T16:23:37.911487+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of these claims.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under standard line
…[truncated 905 chars]

_source line 8577 · 2026-05-19T16:24:11.593922+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: layer selection sensitivity (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of these claims.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under linear residu
…[truncated 1079 chars]

_source line 8583 · 2026-05-19T16:24:24.640895+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\dagger$ are exploratory and the advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (layer selection sensitivity) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\looseness=-1
4

_source line 8607 · 2026-05-19T16:25:28.774571+00:00_

49	\subsection{Evaluation Protocol}
50	\label{sec:evaluation}
51	
52	We report balanced accuracy, precision, pre-CP FPR, coverage, and lead time. We exclude F1, AUROC, and MCC: F1 conflates precision and recall (our coverage metric already captures recall-like behavior at the trace level); AUROC is threshold-free and cannot diagnose the layer selection bias that manifests at fixed decision thresholds; MCC is redundant with BA under balanced class priors and adds no discriminative information for layer selection. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings, selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed across all models as the value balancing coverage ($\geq$70\%) and safe-trace false positive rate ($\leq$50\%); \cref{tab:k_ablation} confirms that the HS vs.\ text conclusion direction is consistent at $K{=}3$, $5$, and $7$, with coverage and FPR trading off monotonically. Modality comparisons use BCa bootstrap (10{,}000 resamples, resampling traces as clusters: each trace is an independent unit and all its steps are included or excluded together, preserving within-trace dependence)~\citep{efron1993bootstrap,diciccio1996bootstrap} with Holm-Bonferroni step-down (3 metrics $\times$ 4 models $= 12$ tests, $\alpha{=}0.05$); the 4-test framework yields identical conclusions (\cref{app:holm_bonferroni}). For 32B models ($n \leq 20$), the exact permutation test is the primary inference method; BCa confidence intervals are reported for comparability but may have reduced coverage at these sample sizes. Within each model, the three metrics are positively correlated, so the correction is stricter than under independence. Control task selectivity~\citep{hewitt2019control} validates genuine structure (\cref{app:control}). $\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \leq 20$): R1-32B ($n{=}20$) and QwQ-32B ($n{=}19$).
53

8	
9	\paragraph{Hyperparameter selection and scope.}
10	Layer (up to 64), $W$ (7 values), $K$ (5 values), and threshold are selected on the same validation set ($n{=}4$--$9$ per-class at 32B, up to $2{,}240$ configurations). HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric tests but not the cumulative researcher degrees of freedom in layer, metric, window, and evidence tier selection; cumulative degrees of freedom are substantial (\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness} provides convergence, cross-validation, and bootstrap analyses addressing this concern). BA-optimal layer indices are sensitive to the train/validation split, spanning 7--98\% depth across models and splits (\cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}); for example, OT-7B shifts from L16 to L2 and QwQ-32B from L60 to L63 between 70/30 and 60/20/20 splits, a direct manifestation of the layer selection sensitivity that constitutes our primary finding. Threshold-based metrics are more stable but still vary by up to 4 layers. $K{=}5$ was selected on the R1-8B validation set and held fixed (\cref{app:k_ablation}); joint (layer, $W$) stability was verified for QwQ-32B (\cref{app:layer_sweep}) but not R1-32B. CP annotations follow HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} without independent IAA evaluation; if annotation error is ${\geq}2$ steps, QwQ-32B's lead time ($-$1.2 to +1.9) falls within noise, though perturbation analysis shows smooth degradation with no cliff effects (\cref{app:cp_perturbation}). Three of four models share the Qwen architecture; only R1-8B (Llama-based) provides cross-architecture evidence. We cover 2 architectures and 2 scales; intermediate scales, diverse base architectures, proprietary models, and multilingual settings are untested. Results are correlational; causal intervention is needed to confirm mechanistic involvement. ### Tool result

_source line 8619 · 2026-05-19T16:26:03.278286+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B FPR is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

_source line 8623 · 2026-05-19T16:26:10.308241+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of these claims.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under linea

49	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the CR-composite layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the threshold-based deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodology, not a genuine property of model scale (\cref{sec:layer-results}).
50	
51	\paragraph{Layer selection stability (LOO-CV).} Leave-one-out cross-validation on the validation set shows moderate consistency for 7--8B models (65--68\% dominant-layer agreement) but lower for 32B, confirming that layer selection instability is particularly acute at small sample sizes. Under pure balanced accuracy, the dominant layer is selected in 68\% (QwQ-32B: L30, 13/19 folds) and 65\% (R1-32B: L47, 13/20 folds) of folds. Alternative selections scatter across neighboring layers (L29, L55--56 for QwQ; L32, L38, L43 for R1-32B), reinforcing the need for multi-metric auditing rather than single-metric layer selection. Threshold-based metrics show tighter convergence to L63 for both 32B models.
52	
53	\paragraph{Joint (layer, $W$) sensitivity.} Under the original 70/30 validation protocol, we evaluate QwQ-32B across 64 layers $\times$ 3 windows ($W \in \{1, 3, 5\}$) (192 configurations) (\cref{tab:joint_layer_w}). L60 is near-optimal: within 2.1pp of the global best at $W{=}3$ and the top-ranked layer at $W{=}5$. (Under 60/20/20, threshold-based metrics select L63; the shift from L60 to L63 across splits itself illustrates layer selection sensitivity.) The optimal layer shifts from middle-deep (L46--47 at $W{=}1$) to deep (L60 at $W{=}5$), which confirms a layer-window interaction. L30, selected by per-fold LOO-CV at $W{=}1$ under balanced accuracy, is suboptimal across all windows (max BA $= 0.769$ vs.\ global best 0.817). Joint (layer, $W$) stability was evaluated via grid search on $W \in \{1, 3, 5\}$ for QwQ

_source line 8637 · 2026-05-19T16:27:14.252059+00:00_

37	\subsection{Layer Selection Sensitivity}
38	\label{sec:layer-sensitivity}
39	
40	Layer selection is a researcher degree of freedom that can reverse modality comparison conclusions. We evaluate five metrics: balanced accuracy (BA), precision, and inverse FPR are standard probing metrics~\citep{belinkov2022probing}; crossing rate and additive composite come from the safety detection literature. A multiplicative composite (BA $\times$ crossing rate) serves as the \emph{crossing-rate composite} (CR-composite) baseline, so named because it incorporates crossing rate in the selection criterion. Metrics evaluating probe performance at a fixed decision threshold (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) form the \emph{threshold-based} selection. This classification is an empirical observation from metric disagreement, not an a priori design choice; the ablation in \cref{tab:layer_selection} is a reproducible empirical fact independent of our framing. Crossing rate inflates at shallow layers where representations are compressed (inter-step cosine distances 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller; \cref{app:cosine_volatility}), so threshold crossings reflect noise rather than signal. This compression is an independently verifiable geometric property of shallow-layer representations, not an artifact of probe training. Precision, inverse FPR, and additive composite all derive from the same $2\times2$ confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same layer, consistent with their shared structure but not mathematically guaranteed (each metric defines a distinct optimization landscape over layers); BA is threshold-free and provides independent confirmation. The ablation diagnoses which \emph{aspect} drives layer selection bias. The effective number of independent tests is four (one per model), reflected in our HB correction. Results in \cref{sec:layer-results}; full per-layer sweeps in \cref{app:layer_sweep}.
41

_source line 8645 · 2026-05-19T16:27:36.134527+00:00_

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff}, a phenomenon our temporal probes target. Recent work further documents adversarial reasoning capabilities and information leakage in reasoning traces~\cite{hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}, providing additional motivation for hidden-state monitoring.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}

_source line 8649 · 2026-05-19T16:27:44.771517+00:00_

13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) share a common confusion matrix and empirically converge to the same deep layer---L63 (98\%) for both 32B models---consistent with their shared structure, though not mathematically guaranteed. Pure balanced accuracy, a threshold-free metric, selects layers that vary across data splits and models (7--98\% depth under 60/20/20; \cref{tab:ba_layer_comparison}), itself demonstrating that layer selection is a consequential methodological choice. The total exploration space spans 5 metrics $\times$ 7 window sizes $\times$ 28--64 layers (model-dependent); HB correction covers the final 12 model-metric tests, not this full search (\cref{app:hyperparameter_robustness}). The mechanism is that shallow layers produce highly compressed representations with small inter-step margins: step-to-step cosine distances are 1.4--2.7$\times$ smaller than at deep layers across all models (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). A linear probe in this compressed space operates near the noise floor, generating frequent threshold crossings that inflate crossing rate independently of discriminative power (\cref{sec:layer-sensitivity}).

_source line 8653 · 2026-05-19T16:27:53.491820+00:00_

64	
65	The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): confirmatory at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (+15.4pp, position-mediated). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust: $p{=}0.044$ (rank 12/12 in the HB step-down, the weakest surviving test), with only 1/5 seeds passing Bonferroni correction (\cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds ($\Delta$Prec std${=}2.0$pp) but its advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).
66	
67	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d$\rightarrow$PCA 384d) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB; \cref{app:e5_mistral}), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).

_source line 8657 · 2026-05-19T16:28:04.188497+00:00_

38	
39	The downstream impact is severe: at CR-composite layers, only the two 7--8B models show significant HS advantage; both 32B models fail. At threshold-based layers, the HS advantage is graded: confirmatory at 7--8B (OT-7B, R1-8B; nested CV validated), significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (residualized gap below MDE). The $\dagger$ marks sample-limited test sets ($n \leq 20$) where effect sizes are descriptive rather than confirmatory. Had we reported only CR-composite-layer results, we would have concluded a scale-dependent HS advantage that is entirely a methodological artifact. A random-label control on QwQ-32B confirms that the crossing-rate shallow-layer bias is a geometric property of the representations (100\% of random-label repeats select shallow layers), while threshold-based deep-layer selection requires real safety labels (\cref{app:random_cp}). AUROC, excluded from our primary metrics because it is threshold-free (\cref{sec:evaluation}), confirms HS discriminability at BA-selected layers ($\Delta$AUROC +12.1 to +15.1pp; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}) but cannot diagnose the threshold-dependent layer selection bias.

[Director 指令] [Patch] N6 Multi-Metric Layer Ensemble 结果写入论文

## 实验结果

4/4 模型 ensemble HS vs text 均显著（p ≤ 0.002）。Ensemble 从未显著差于 best single metric（p > 0.27）。

| Model | Ensemble Prec | Best Single | Δ | Text Prec | Ens−Text |
|-------|--------------|-------------|---|-----------|----------|
| R1-8B (Llama) | 0.565 | 0.604 (BA@L12) | −3.9pp | 0.372 | **+19.3pp** |
| OT-7B (Qwen-7B) | 0.506 | 0.473 (FPR@L18) | +3.3pp | 0.236 | **+27.0pp** |
| QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B) | 0.578 | 0.578 (Prec@L60) | −0.0pp | 0.374 | **+20.3pp** |
| R1-32B (Qwen-32B) | 0.581 | 0.583 (Prec@L63) | −0.2pp | 0.476 | **+10.5pp** |

方法：每个模型取 3 个 threshold-based metrics（precision / BA / FPR）各自选的 best layer，在这 3 层上分别训练 LogReg，对概率取平均。

## Framing 要求（严格遵守，Reviewer 审查确认）

1. **不说** "mitigates metric-induced bias"（因为 ensemble 只包含 threshold-based metrics，没有测试 composite/crossing-rate）
2. **说** "provides a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled (threshold-based) metrics"
3. **诚实报告** ensemble ≈ best single（不夸大 ensemble 的价值）——关键信息是"不需要预先选定最佳 metric，ensemble 自动达到同等水平"
4. **Future work** 中提及 "including biased metrics (e.g., crossing-rate composite) in the ensemble, or learned metric weighting"

## 放置位置

- **Discussion section**：在已有的 "Guidance for future research" 段落中或其后，加一个 proof-of-concept 小段（~80-100 words）。报告 Table（可以 inline 或小 table）。
- **不要**放在 main Results（这是 proof-of-concept，不是 core finding）
- **Abstract/Conclusion**：各加一句——"A simple three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage indistinguishable from the best individual metric (4/4 models, p ≤ 0.002)."

## 页面限制

仍然必须保持主文本 8 页。N6 内容约 100 words + 小 table。如果放不下 table，用 inline 数字报告核心结果，详细 table 放 Appendix。

## 完成后

git commit，汇报修改位置。

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of these claims.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}

_source line 8681 · 2026-05-19T16:38:14.265834+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\dagger$ are exploratory and the advantage narrows as text encoders improve (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (metric-induced layer selection bias) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\looseness=-1
4

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control (a sensitivity analysis, not the primary specification), only 2/4 remain significant---QwQ-32B's advantage is absorbed by the $(t/T)^2$ term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). The absorption could also reflect quadratic overfitting at small $n$ (QwQ-32B mean $T{\\approx}51$ steps provides adequate degrees of freedom for two parameters, but with $n{=}19$ traces the quadratic term may capture spurious variance); the difference nonetheless motivates caution for the 32B results. Our work diagnoses the layer selection problem; \\citet{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} propose a complementary solution via multi-layer ensembling that bypasses single-layer brittleness. We propose---but have not validated---a multi-metric audit protocol: compute $\\geq$3 metrics (threshold-free and threshold-based), flag metric disagreement, and verify cosine volatility at candidate layers (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\\looseness=-1",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}\nConverging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control (a sensitivity analysis, not the primary specification), only 2/4 remain significant---QwQ-32B's advantage is absorbed by the $(t/T)^2$ term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), possibly reflecting quadratic overfitting at $n{=}19$ (though per-trace mean $T{\\approx}51$ provides adequate DOF). We propose a multi-metric audit protocol: compute ${\\geq}3$ metrics, flag disagreement, and verify cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}). Raising $K$ yields low-FPR operating points ($K{=}10$: 8\\% FPR at 21\\% coverage; \\cref{app:deployment_operating_points}). Broader validation across architectures, datasets, and attack types is needed before deployment.\\looseness=-1\n\n\\paragraph{Layer ensemble proof of concept.}\nTraining probes at the best layer selected by each threshold-based metric (precision, BA, FPR) and averaging predicted probabilities yields a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled metrics: HS vs.\\ text precision is significant for all four models ($p \\leq 0.002$; \\cref{tab:ensemble}), with +10.5 to +27.0pp advantages. The ensemble never significantly underperforms the best individual metric ($p > 0.27$), eliminating the need to pre-select which metric to optimize. Including biased metrics (e.g., crossing-rate composite) or learned weighting~\\citep{pais2026linear_probe_scaling} remains future work.\\looseness=-1"
}
```

</details>
