```json
{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex",
  "old_string": "At 32B scale, the MDE is 8.6--9.5pp, meaning effects below this magnitude cannot be reliably distinguished from noise. R1-32B's residualized HS-text gap (+1.9pp) falls well below the MDE (9.5pp), so the non-significant result (\\cref{app:position_residual}) is consistent with either a true null or an underpowered test. The 7--8B MDEs (3.3--4.2pp) are substantially lower, explaining why smaller effects reach significance at these scales.",
  "new_string": "At 32B scale, the MDE is 8.6--9.5pp, meaning effects below this magnitude cannot be reliably distinguished from noise. R1-32B's residualized HS-text gap (+1.9pp) falls well below the MDE (9.5pp), so the non-significant result (\\cref{app:position_residual}) is consistent with either a true null or an underpowered test. The 7--8B MDEs (3.3--4.2pp) are substantially lower, explaining why smaller effects reach significance at these scales.\n\n\\subsection{Multi-Metric Layer Ensemble}\n\\label{app:ensemble}\n\nFor each model, we train logistic regression probes at the best layer selected by each of three threshold-based metrics (precision, BA, inverse FPR) and average their predicted probabilities to produce an ensemble score. This eliminates the need to pre-select a single metric for layer selection.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Multi-metric layer ensemble vs.\\ best single metric and text baseline. Ensemble trains probes at 3 threshold-based metric-selected layers and averages probabilities. $\\Delta$Ens--Best: ensemble minus best single metric (negative = ensemble slightly lower). All Ens--Text differences are significant ($p \\leq 0.002$, permutation test). No Ens--Best difference is significant ($p > 0.27$).}\n\\label{tab:ensemble}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Model} & \\textbf{Ens Prec} & \\textbf{Best Single} & $\\Delta$ & \\textbf{Ens$-$Text} \\\\\n\\midrule\nR1-8B & 0.565 & 0.604 (BA@L12) & $-$3.9pp & +19.3pp \\\\\nOT-7B & 0.506 & 0.473 (FPR@L18) & +3.3pp & +27.0pp \\\\\nQwQ-32B & 0.578 & 0.578 (Prec@L60) & $-$0.0pp & +20.3pp \\\\\nR1-32B & 0.581 & 0.583 (Prec@L63) & $-$0.2pp & +10.5pp \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe ensemble provides a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled (threshold-based) metrics. It never significantly underperforms the best individual metric (all $p > 0.27$), and all four models show significant HS advantage over text ($p \\leq 0.002$). The ensemble advantage over text ranges from +10.5pp (R1-32B) to +27.0pp (OT-7B). This proof of concept validates that practitioners need not commit to a single metric for layer selection; averaging across metric-selected layers achieves comparable performance. Future work should test whether including biased metrics (e.g., crossing-rate composite) in the ensemble degrades performance, and whether learned metric weighting could improve upon uniform averaging."
```
```json
}
```

     1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
     2	
     3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
     4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}) and likely generalizable to any threshold-based detection study. This separates the two contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry regardless of sample size and is likely to generalize beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent, and its magnitude may not transfer to other benchmarks or annotation schemes. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
     5	
     6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
     7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage represents information not yet captured by current sentence encoders rather than a fundamental modality difference~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger future encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} could close the gap entirely bounds the shelf life of these claims.\looseness=-1
     8	
     9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
    10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal

_source line 8741 · 2026-05-19T16:47:03.027625+00:00_

     1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
     2	
     3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\dagger$ are exploratory and narrow with stronger encoders (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (metric-induced layer selection bias) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. A three-layer ensemble validates metric-agnostic selection among threshold-based metrics (4/4 significant, $p \leq 0.002$). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\looseness=-1

_source line 8743 · 2026-05-19T16:47:17.292656+00:00_

     1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
     2	
     3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
     4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}). This separates the two contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent and may not transfer to other benchmarks. The threshold-based layers are the deepest in the network (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
     5	
     6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
     7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), providing partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage reflects information not yet in current sentence encoders~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\looseness=-1
     8	
     9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
    10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control (a sensitivity analysis, not the primary specification), only 2/4

     1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
     2	
     3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\dagger$ are exploratory and narrow with stronger encoders (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (metric-induced layer selection bias) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. A three-layer ensemble validates metric-agnostic selection among threshold-based metrics (4/4 significant, $p \leq 0.002$). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\looseness=-1\looseness=-1

@@ -1 +1 @@
-Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
+Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generati

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	Reasoning models such as DeepSeek-R1~\cite{deepseekr1} and QwQ~\cite{qwq} scale inference-time computation through extended chains of thought (CoT). Recent work documents that these models can ``self-jailbreak'': without adversarial input modification, the CoT transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation~\cite{harmthoughts,selfjailbreak}. We define the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases as the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP). This failure mode differs qualitatively from adversarial jailbreaks on standard LLMs because the harmful transition originates within the model's own reasoning. Before building production detectors, a prior question must be answered: can current probing methods reliably distinguish pre-CP from post-CP reasoning steps, and under what conditions? This paper provides a systematic characterization of what hidden-state and text-based probes can and cannot detect.
4	
5	Existing safety detectors target fundamentally different problems. Hidden-state (HS) methods such as TrajGuard~\cite{trajguard} and SafeSwitch~\cite{safeswitch} classify adversarial inputs at the token level, while text monitors like SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} scan generated reasoning for explicit violations. Neither tracks the gradual, multi-step commitment process. Before asking which modality is better, a prior methodological question must be answered: how sensitive are probe conclusions to the choice of layer selection metric? HS probes require choosing which transformer layer to monitor, a researcher degree of freedom whose metric sensitivity has received almost no scrutiny. We show this choice alone can reverse a study's central conclusion.
6

…[truncated 2635 chars]

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}). This separates the two contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent and may not transfer to other benchmarks. Threshold-based layers are the deepest (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (confirmatory at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage reflects information not yet in current sentence encoders~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal} or ITI~\citep{li2024iti} is needed. 1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is confirmatory (nested CV validated); the 32B results$^\dagger$ are exploratory and narrow with stronger encoders (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (metric-induced layer selection bias) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. A three-layer ensemble validates metric-agnostic selection (4/4 significant, $p \leq 0.002$). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\looseness=-1
4

### Tool result

_source line 8785 · 2026-05-19T18:13:24.734922+00:00_

1	Reasoning models with chain-of-thought (CoT) can self-jailbreak: their reasoning traces transition from safe deliberation to committed harmful generation without adversarial input. Detecting the commitment point (CP)---the first reasoning step after which safety deliberation permanently ceases---requires monitoring internal representations or generated text. We study four reasoning LLMs, primarily Qwen-based (3/4 models) with one Llama model for limited cross-architecture evidence, on HarmThoughts and provide initial characterization of hidden-state (HS) vs.\ text probe capabilities on this benchmark. The comparison depends critically on which transformer layer is monitored: a crossing-rate composite metric masks the HS advantage at 32B scale by selecting shallow layers where representation compression inflates threshold crossings. Threshold-based layer selection recovers HS precision gains of +19.2 to +21.9pp against 384d MiniLM (Holm-Bonferroni corrected); this advantage diminishes with stronger encoders (384d: 4/4 models pass HB $\to$ 1024d BGE-large: 1/4). The 7--8B results are confirmatory (nested CV validated): precision and balanced accuracy confirmed; R1-8B safe-trace false positive rate (FPR) is significant but seed-sensitive (3/5 seeds pass HB). The 32B results are exploratory$^\dagger$ ($^\dagger$\,sample-limited, $n \leq 20$): QwQ-32B is significant but position-competitive; R1-32B is position-mediated and below minimum detectable effect. A three-layer ensemble provides metric-agnostic HS advantage matching the best individual metric (4/4 models, $p \leq 0.002$). Metric-induced layer selection bias is the robust methodological finding; the empirical HS advantage is conditional on encoder quality, model scale, and position control.
2

1	\section{Limitations}\label{sec:limitations}
2	
3	\paragraph{Single dataset.}
4	All experiments use one dataset (HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts}), which provides step-level CP annotations derived from manual inspection of reasoning chains. The dataset spans multiple harmful categories (e.g., violence, fraud, illegal activities) but we do not stratify results by category; categories with longer average reasoning chains or earlier CPs may exhibit different probe dynamics. CP positions range from early to late within traces, though the distribution is right-skewed (most CPs occur in the first half of reasoning). Annotation granularity (step-level vs.\ token-level), category distribution, and annotator team composition may all influence which layers carry discriminative signal and how strongly metric bias manifests. Inter-annotator agreement (IAA) was not computed for these annotations. Random annotation noise would attenuate effect sizes symmetrically, making our estimates conservative; systematic noise (e.g., consistent early or late CP placement) could bias layer selection or inflate modality differences. Without IAA, we cannot distinguish these cases, so our conclusions may not transfer to independently annotated corpora. Generalization to other jailbreak datasets, attack taxonomies, or multilingual settings is untested.
5	
6	\paragraph{Model-specific caveats and statistical power.}
7	Current probes characterize the commitment phenomenon rather than constitute production detectors: false positive rates remain 11--50\% (model-dependent), and the 76.7\% oracle gap (R1-8B; \cref{sec:oracle}) underscores how much room remains. R1-32B achieves significance only at $W{=}1$ (25\% coverage, 5/20 traces) and its HS advantage is position-mediated (residualized gap +1.9pp, $p{=}0.914$; \cref{app:position_residual}). QwQ-32B's position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ 0.796) with negative HS lead time. R1-8B FPR significance is seed-fragile (3/5 HB). A

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{confirmatory}\footnote{In our usage, \emph{confirmatory} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \emph{Exploratory} ($\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.
10	
11	\subsection{Layer Selection Results}\label{sec:layer-results}
12	
13	\cref{tab:layer_selection} shows that the crossing-rate composite metric (balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate) systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models: L2 (3\% depth) for QwQ-32B and L10 (16\%) for R1-32B. The three threshold-based metrics (precision, inv
…[truncated 13337 chars]

1	\section{Related Work}\label{sec:related-work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Safety in Reasoning Models.}
4	Reasoning models introduce a qualitatively new failure mode: the model's own chain of thought can transition from safety-aware deliberation to harmful generation without adversarial input modification~\cite{wang2025lrmsafety}.
5	
6	\textit{Attack characterization.} Self-jailbreak has been documented with step-level annotations~\cite{harmthoughts}, with longer chains increasing circumvention probability~\cite{selfjailbreak}. H-CoT~\cite{hcot2025} shows reasoning chains can be externally hijacked. Unfaithful reasoning~\cite{chen2025unfaithful,turpin2023language} further motivates hidden-state monitoring that bypasses text. Safety-aligned models maintain refusal intentions during thinking but experience a sharp drop at output generation~\cite{yin2025refusal_cliff,hagendorff2026lrm_jailbreak,hu2026refusal_dynamics,ahrend2026safer_traces}.
7	
8	\textit{Detection.} CoT monitoring can detect reward hacking but optimization pressure promotes obfuscation~\cite{baker2025monitoring}. CoT monitorability is valuable but fragile~\cite{korbak2025cot_monitorability}. GuardReasoner~\cite{xiang2025guardreasoner} trains a dedicated reasoning-based guard model.
9	
10	\textit{Mitigation.} SafeThink~\cite{safethink} steers early reasoning toward safety, SafeRemind~\cite{saferemind} inserts entropy-triggered reminders, and Chain-of-Guardrails~\cite{mao2025selfjailbreak_reasoning} proposes guardrail insertion during reasoning. None of these conducts a systematic modality comparison for commitment detection.
11	
12	\paragraph{Hidden-State Safety Detection.}
13	Internal activations can detect safety violations in standard LLMs. \citet{azaria2023internal} demonstrate that internal states encode whether a model's output is truthful, motivating representation-based monitoring. \citet{zhou2024alignment} show safety-relevant information distributes across layers, while \citet{qi2025shallow_alignment} f
…[truncated 4671 chars]

1	\section{Problem Setup and Methods}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{Safety Commitment Point}
5	\label{sec:commitment-point}
6	
7	When a reasoning model is jailbroken, its chain-of-thought (CoT) transitions from safety-aware deliberation to committed harmful generation. We define the \textbf{Safety Commitment Point} (CP) as the first reasoning step after which safety reasoning permanently ceases; traces with oscillating safety deliberation are resolved by taking the final irreversible transition (\cref{sec:limitations}). Steps before CP are \emph{pre-commitment} ($y_t = 0$); steps at or after CP are \emph{post-commitment} ($y_t = 1$). The detection task produces a score $s_t \in [0, 1]$ estimating whether the model has passed its CP by step $t$.
8	
9	We study four reasoning models spanning two architectures and two scales (\cref{tab:models}).
10	
11	\begin{table}[t]
12	\centering
13	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
14	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
15	\toprule
16	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{Arch.} & \textbf{Params} & \textbf{Layers} & \textbf{Hidden} & \textbf{$n$ (total\,/\,test)} \\
17	\midrule
18	R1-8B & Llama & 8B & 32 & 4096 & 209\,/\,43 \\
19	OT-7B & Qwen & 7B & 28 & 3584 & 228\,/\,47 \\
20	R1-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 100\,/\,20 \\
21	QwQ-32B & Qwen & 32B & 64 & 5120 & 95\,/\,19 \\
22	\bottomrule
23	\end{tabular}}
24	\caption{Models studied. R1-8B and R1-32B are distilled from DeepSeek-R1~\citep{deepseekr1}; QwQ-32B~\citep{qwq} is a native reasoning model; OT-7B~\citep{openthinker} is trained with STILL~\citep{still} on Qwen2.5-7B. Data: HarmThoughts~\citep{harmthoughts} with step-level CP annotations. Split: 60/20/20.}
25	\label{tab:models}
26	\end{table}
27	
28	\subsection{Probe Architecture}
29	\label{sec:probe-architecture}
30	
31	\paragraph{Hidden-state (HS) probes.} At each step $t$, we extract the last-token hidden state $\mathbf{h}_t^{(\ell)} \in \mathbb{R}^d$ from layer $\ell$ and train an L2-regularized logistic regression classifier (balanced class 
…[truncated 5016 chars]

```json
  "old_string": "Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control (a sensitivity analysis, not the primary specification), only 2/4 remain significant---QwQ-32B's advantage is absorbed by the $(t/T)^2$ term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}), possibly reflecting overfitting at $n{=}19$.",
  "new_string": "Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 remain significant---QwQ-32B's advantage is absorbed by the $(t/T)^2$ term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Linear residualization is the conventional default; quadratic control risks overfitting at $n{=}17$--$20$ (two additional parameters consume scarce degrees of freedom), so the divergent results bracket the true position contribution. That HS probes may partly function as better position encoders than text probes remains an unexcluded alternative."
}
```

_source line 8867 · 2026-05-19T18:18:50.136783+00:00_

417	\paragraph{Position-only baseline.} As a complementary validation, we train a logistic regression classifier using only the normalized step index ($t / T$ where $T$ is the total trace length) as the input feature. This baseline tests whether temporal position alone explains probe performance. On R1-8B, the position-only baseline produces 15.8\% FPR compared to 8.1\% for the HS probe at matched detection rates. The probe's substantially lower FPR confirms that hidden state features carry information beyond what step position provides.
418	
419	\paragraph{Residualized selectivity.} To confirm that selectivity survives position removal, we recompute control task selectivity on position-regressed features (the same residualized representations used in \cref{app:position_residual}). Residualized selectivity: R1-8B 22.9pp, OT-7B 10.4pp, QwQ-32B 11.3pp (all ${>}5$pp), R1-32B 3.0pp (${<}5$pp, consistent with the position-mediated advantage reported in \cref{sec:hs-text}). Three of four models retain selectivity well above the \citet{hewitt2019control} threshold after position removal; R1-32B's drop confirms that its safety signal is largely positional.
420	
421

### Tool result

_source line 8869 · 2026-05-19T18:18:50.589130+00:00_

595	\paragraph{Issue.} In experiment M002 (text baseline evaluation), the QwQ-32B text probe was inadvertently initialized with a midpoint heuristic placeholder rather than true sentence embeddings from all-MiniLM-L6-v2. Specifically, the placeholder assigned each step a feature vector equal to the midpoint between the mean safe and mean unsafe embeddings from the training set, with small Gaussian noise added. This made the text probe trivially predictive and produced a spuriously significant result ($p{=}0.001$ for HS vs. text FPR difference).
596	
597	\paragraph{Discovery and correction.} The bug was identified during a systematic audit of the feature extraction pipeline. After correction (using actual sentence embeddings), the QwQ-32B and R1-32B modality comparisons at the originally selected layers (L2 and L10, chosen by the CR-composite metric) showed no significant HS advantage: QwQ-32B accuracy $p{=}0.828$, precision $p{=}0.346$; R1-32B precision $p{=}0.050$ but no tests surviving Holm-Bonferroni. The subsequent discovery that these layer selections were themselves artifacts of metric bias (\cref{sec:layer-results}) led to the threshold-based layer analysis reported in the main text, where the HS advantage follows an evidence gradient at threshold-based deep layers: confirmed at 7--8B, significant but position-competitive at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$, and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}).
598	
599	\paragraph{Impact on reported results.} All numbers reported in this paper use the corrected text embedding pipeline. The bug affected only QwQ-32B text probe features. The correction, combined with the separate layer selection fix (\cref{sec:layer-results}), means that early-stage intermediate results (non-significant 32B modality comparisons) reflected two compounding errors: incorrect text features and CR-composite layer selection. Both are fully resolved in the final analysis.

_source line 8871 · 2026-05-19T18:18:50.891212+00:00_

797	\end{table}
798	
799	Position accounts for 9--20pp of balanced accuracy depending on model and window size (R1-32B's 20.4pp drop at $W{=}1$ reflects the absence of temporal context), and all models retain non-positional signal above chance ($p{<}0.001$). Three of four models retain significant non-positional HS advantage over text after residualization (OT-7B +6.9pp, R1-8B +6.4pp at $W{=}15$, QwQ-32B +3.7pp; all HB corrected). R1-32B is the exception: residualized HS-text gap is +1.9pp ($p{=}0.914$), so its HS advantage is position-mediated. Larger temporal windows reduce position dependence: R1-8B drops 24.3pp at $W{=}3$ but only 16.5pp at $W{=}15$.
800	
801	For QwQ-32B specifically, three complementary significance tests confirm the residualized probe performs above chance: Wilcoxon signed-rank $p{=}0.0024$, paired $t$-test $p{=}0.0008$, permutation test $p{<}0.001$ (bootstrap 95\% CI for per-trace BA: $[0.575, 0.744]$). The position baseline achieves higher balanced accuracy (0.832 vs.\ HS 0.796), and HS precision (+4.5pp, $p{=}0.133$) and FPR (+2.5pp, $p{=}0.279$) margins over the position baseline are not statistically significant at $n{=}19$.

_source line 8881 · 2026-05-19T18:19:13.956056+00:00_

63	\end{table}
64	
65	The HS advantage is encoder-conditional: against BGE-large (1024d), only OT-7B survives HB correction (\cref{app:encoder_robustness}). Multi-seed stability appears in \cref{app:multi_seed}. Against the 384d baseline, HS probes outperform text probes in all four models$^\dagger$ (\cref{tab:hs_vs_text}): cross-validated at 7--8B (OT-7B: +21.9pp precision, R1-8B: +20.1pp), significant but suggestive of position-competitiveness at QwQ-32B$^\dagger$ (+19.2pp), and exploratory at R1-32B$^\dagger$ (+15.4pp, pattern consistent with position mediation). R1-8B FPR is directionally consistent (5/5 seeds) but not robust ($p{=}0.044$, rank 12/12 in HB step-down; 1/5 seeds Bonferroni; \cref{app:multi_seed}). R1-32B passes 5/5 seeds but is position-mediated (residualized gap NS; \cref{app:position_residual}); QwQ-32B's position baseline is competitive (\cref{sec:32b-analysis}). Overall, 18/20 model-seed combinations pass full HB (\cref{app:multi_seed}). Control task selectivity confirms genuine safety signal: 11.1--16.8pp above chance (\cref{app:control}).\looseness=-1
66	
67	\paragraph{Capacity control.} PCA reduction of HS features to 384d (79--92\% variance retained) preserves the precision advantage across all four models$^\dagger$ (+16.8 to +23.1pp, all $p{<}0.012$, all HB). The control provides partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Random projection to 384d yields similar results ($\Delta$Prec +8.3 to +20.5pp; \cref{app:random_projection}), further weakening the capacity explanation. E5-Mistral-7B (4096d$\rightarrow$PCA 384d) yields intermediate results (2/4 HB; \cref{app:e5_mistral}), forming a monotonic encoder-quality gradient with MiniLM (4/4) and BGE-large (1/4).

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom~\citep{gelman2013garden}.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows that crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer representation compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed as label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}). This separates the two contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) follows from representation geometry and likely generalizes beyond HarmThoughts; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample- and dataset-dependent and may not transfer to other benchmarks. Threshold-based layers are the deepest (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; however, R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (cross-validated at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. The HS advantage reflects information not yet in current sentence encoders~\citep{boxo2025linear_probes}; whether stronger encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 remain significant---QwQ-32B's advantage is absorbed by the $(

_source line 8903 · 2026-05-19T18:22:29.166640+00:00_

1	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
2	
3	Layer selection is a critical researcher degree of freedom that can reverse a study's central conclusion; the multi-metric audit protocol (\cref{sec:discussion}) would have prevented our initial spurious finding. The 7--8B HS advantage is cross-validated (nested CV); the 32B results$^\dagger$ are exploratory and narrow with stronger encoders (1/4 HB at 1024d). Contribution~1 (metric-induced layer selection bias) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage magnitude) is initial characterization on one benchmark requiring further validation. A three-layer ensemble validates metric-agnostic selection (4/4 significant, $p \leq 0.002$). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are immediate next steps.\looseness=-1
4

```json
{
```
```json
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/contrastive_trace_safety/docs/paper/sections/discussion.tex",
```
```json
  "old_string": "Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 remain significant---QwQ-32B's advantage is absorbed by the $(t/T)^2$ term (\\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Linear residualization is the conventional default; quadratic control risks overfitting at $n{=}17$--$20$ (two additional parameters consume scarce degrees of freedom), so the divergent results bracket the true position contribution. That HS probes may partly function as better position encoders than text probes remains an unexcluded alternative. We propose a multi-metric audit protocol: compute ${\\geq}3$ metrics, flag disagreement, and verify cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility,app:deployment_operating_points}).\\looseness=-1",
  "new_string": "Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 (QwQ-32B absorbed by $(t/T)^2$; \\cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Quadratic control risks overfitting at $n{=}17$--$20$, so the divergent results bracket the true position contribution; HS probes partly functioning as better position encoders remains unexcluded. We propose a multi-metric audit protocol: compute ${\\geq}3$ metrics, flag disagreement, and verify cosine volatility (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility,app:deployment_operating_points}).\\looseness=-1"
}
```

```json
  "old_string": "Training probes at the best layer selected by each threshold-based metric (precision, BA, FPR) and averaging predicted probabilities yields a robust, metric-agnostic selection strategy among principled metrics: HS vs.\\ text precision is significant for all four models ($p \\leq 0.002$; \\cref{tab:ensemble}), with +10.5 to +27.0pp advantages. Contribution~1 (layer selection bias) generalizes from representation geometry; Contribution~2 (HS advantage) requires validation on additional benchmarks. A three-layer ensemble validates metric-agnostic selection (4/4 significant, $p \\leq 0.002$). Causal validation and LLM-based encoders~\\citep{wang2024e5mistral,li2024iti} are next steps.\\looseness=-1"
}
```

_source line 8963 · 2026-05-19T21:50:31.784204+00:00_

[Director 指令] [Patch] R1-8B 全层 Layer Sweep 结果写入论文

## 新实验结果

R1-8B 在 HarmThoughts 上用全部 25 层（L0-L24）跑了 5-metric × 4-window 完整 sweep。之前只有 L12-L24（13 层），现在补全了 L0-L11 浅层。

核心发现：
- **crossing_rate 在所有 window 下都选 L0**（极端浅层偏好）
- 其他 metric（precision/BA/FPR/composite）选 L9-L16
- 同一模型上，不同 metric 的层选择差距达 **16 层**（L0 vs L16）
- HS 在所有层所有 window 下均显著优于 Text（gap ~0.15-0.17）

具体数值（composite metric, 4 windows）：
- W=1: best L16 (0.789), shallow mean 0.746 vs deep mean 0.762
- W=3: best L12 (0.796)
- W=5: best L12 (0.803)
- W=15: best L9 (0.784), **shallow bias 出现** — shallow mean 0.748 > deep mean 0.745

## 写入位置

### Section 4（Experiments）
在 Table 2 讨论后或 metric-bias 分析段落中加 1-2 句：
"Full-layer analysis of R1-8B (L0–L24; previously limited to L12–L24) reveals that crossing-rate selects L0 across all window sizes, while threshold-based metrics consistently select mid-to-deep layers (L9–L16)—a 16-layer divergence within a single model (Table X in Appendix)."

### Appendix
新增 Table：R1-8B Full-Layer Sweep Results
- 5 metrics × 4 windows × 25 layers 的 top-1 layer + score
- 或简化为 5 metrics × 4 windows 的 best-layer 表格（不需要全 25 层数值）

### Discussion
在 "metric-induced systematic bias" 段落中加：
"Extended to the full layer range, crossing-rate's shallow-layer preference persists at L0—the shallowest possible layer—while threshold-based metrics remain in the L9–L16 range, confirming that the bias is not an artifact of the originally restricted layer range (L12–L24)."

## Framing
- 这是对现有 Contribution 1 的加强证据，不是新 claim
- 强调"补全浅层后 metric-bias pattern 更加极端"
- 不夸大——crossing_rate 选 L0 是因为浅层表征压缩导致 crossing events 多，这个机制在论文中已有解释

## 页面限制
主文本加 1-2 句（~30 words），详细 table 放 Appendix。应该不影响 8 页限制。

完成后 git commit，汇报修改位置。

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	We evaluate temporal probes across four reasoning models (\cref{tab:models}), comparing hidden-state (HS) and text modalities under different layer selection criteria.
4	
5	\paragraph{Evidence gradient.} We classify HS advantage evidence into three tiers: \emph{cross-validated}\footnote{In our usage, \emph{cross-validated} means the result survives nested 5-fold cross-validation with a held-out test fold, ruling out hyperparameter overfitting. \emph{Exploratory} ($\dagger$) results pass HB on the holdout split but lack nested CV validation due to sample constraints.} (nested CV pass; 7--8B models), \emph{significant} (HB pass without nested CV; QwQ-32B), and \emph{exploratory} ($^\dagger$, small sample or narrow coverage; R1-32B). Per-model details follow in \cref{sec:layer-results,sec:hs-text,sec:32b-analysis}.
6	
7	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
8	
9	We study R1-8B (Llama-8B, R1-distilled, $n{=}43$ test traces), OT-7B (OpenThinker-7B, trained on the OpenThoughts dataset~\citep{openthinker}; Qwen-7B backbone, STILL-trained, $n{=}47$), QwQ-32B (Qwen-32B, native reasoning, $n{=}19$--$20$), and R1-32B (Qwen-32B, R1-distilled, $n{=}20$). All traces come from HarmThoughts with step-level CP annotations (60/20/20 split). HS probes extract last-token hidden states from a selected layer; text probes encode each step with all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (384d). Both use L2-regularized logistic regression with temporal windowing $W \in \{1, 3, 5, 10, 15, 20, 25\}$ selected on validation. Detection requires $K{=}5$ consecutive threshold crossings. Statistical testing follows \cref{sec:evaluation}.

1	\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	\paragraph{Layer selection as researcher degree of freedom~\citep{gelman2013garden}.}
4	Our five-metric ablation (\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed label-independent (\cref{app:random_cp}). This separates the contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) generalizes from representation geometry; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample-dependent. Threshold-based layers are the deepest (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \citet{hewitt2019control}.\looseness=-1
5	
6	\paragraph{Encoder-quality boundary.}
7	The HS advantage is significant against 384d MiniLM (cross-validated at 7--8B; exploratory at 32B) but attenuated against 1024d BGE-large (1/4 HB). PCA capacity matching preserves significance (+16.8 to +23.1pp), partial evidence against a pure capacity explanation. Whether stronger encoders~\citep{wang2024e5mistral,boxo2025linear_probes} close the gap bounds these claims' shelf life.\looseness=-1
8	
9	\paragraph{Guidance for future research.}
10	Converging evidence (oracle analysis, PCA capacity control, control task selectivity, position residualization) supports genuine safety representations, but causal validation via DAS~\citep{geiger2025causal}, ITI~\citep{li2024iti}, or circuit breakers~\citep{zou2024improving} is needed. Under linear residualization, 3/4 models retain significant non-positional HS advantage; under quadratic control, only 2/4 (QwQ-32B absorbed by $(t/T)^2$; \cref{tab:position_quadratic}). Quadratic control risks overfitting at $n{=}17$--$20$, so the divergent results bracket the true position contribution; HS probes partly functioning as better position encoders remains unexcluded. 42	\section{Layer Sweep Results}
43	\label{app:layer_sweep}
44	
45	For each model, we sweep all transformer layers and evaluate five candidate selection metrics: (1)~balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate (the CR-composite), (2)~pure balanced accuracy, (3)~precision, (4)~inverse pre-CP FPR, and (5)~an additive composite. The CR-composite systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models because early-layer representations exhibit higher crossing rates (more frequent threshold crossings) regardless of discriminative power. Three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) converge on deep layers for all models; pure balanced accuracy selects layers whose depth varies with the data split (under 70/30: L30 for QwQ-32B, L47 for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20: L63 and L46 respectively; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}).
46	
47	\paragraph{Selected layers.} R1-8B: all five metrics agree on layer~14 (44\% depth). OT-7B: CR-composite selects L0 (0\%), threshold-based consensus selects L16 (57\%). QwQ-32B: CR-composite selects L2 (3\%), threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98\%). R1-32B: CR-composite selects L10 (16\%), threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98\%). The disagreement is most extreme for 64-layer models, where the CR-composite selects layers in the bottom 16\% of the network while threshold-based metrics select layers at 98\% depth. Under 70/30, pure balanced accuracy selects L30 (47\%) for QwQ-32B and L47 (73\%) for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20, it selects L63 (98\%) and L46 (72\%), which demonstrates sensitivity to the data split.
48	
49	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the CR-composite layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the threshold-based deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodolo

_source line 8975 · 2026-05-19T21:53:58.658127+00:00_

42	\section{Layer Sweep Results}
43	\label{app:layer_sweep}
44	
45	For each model, we sweep all transformer layers and evaluate five candidate selection metrics: (1)~balanced accuracy $\times$ crossing rate (the CR-composite), (2)~pure balanced accuracy, (3)~precision, (4)~inverse pre-CP FPR, and (5)~an additive composite. The CR-composite systematically selects shallow layers for 32B models because early-layer representations exhibit higher crossing rates (more frequent threshold crossings) regardless of discriminative power. Three threshold-based metrics (precision, inverse FPR, additive composite) converge on deep layers for all models; pure balanced accuracy selects layers whose depth varies with the data split (under 70/30: L30 for QwQ-32B, L47 for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20: L63 and L46 respectively; \cref{tab:ba_layer_results}).
46	
47	\paragraph{Selected layers.} R1-8B: all five metrics agree on layer~14 (44\% depth). OT-7B: CR-composite selects L0 (0\%), threshold-based consensus selects L16 (57\%). QwQ-32B: CR-composite selects L2 (3\%), threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98\%). R1-32B: CR-composite selects L10 (16\%), threshold-based consensus selects L63 (98\%). The disagreement is most extreme for 64-layer models, where the CR-composite selects layers in the bottom 16\% of the network while threshold-based metrics select layers at 98\% depth. Under 70/30, pure balanced accuracy selects L30 (47\%) for QwQ-32B and L47 (73\%) for R1-32B; under the canonical 60/20/20, it selects L63 (98\%) and L46 (72\%), which demonstrates sensitivity to the data split.
48	
49	\paragraph{Impact on modality comparison.} At the CR-composite layers, HS vs.\ text precision $p$-values are 0.064 (R1-32B) and 0.35--0.83 (QwQ-32B), both non-significant. At the threshold-based deep layers, all $p$-values fall below 0.005. The initially reported ``scale-dependent'' pattern (HS advantage at 7--8B but not 32B) was entirely an artifact of layer selection methodology, not

71	\subsection{Random-Label Decomposition Control}
72	\label{app:random_cp}
73	
74	To decompose the sources of metric disagreement in layer selection, we replace the true CP annotations with uniformly random labels and re-run the full layer sweep on QwQ-32B (95 traces, 64 layers, 10 random-label repeats). \cref{tab:random_cp} reports the fraction of repeats selecting a shallow layer (L$\leq$10) and the mean selected layer.

```json
  "old_string": "Our five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}). This separates the contributions: metric-induced layer selection bias (Contribution~1) generalizes from representation geometry; the empirical HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample-dependent. Threshold-based layers are the deepest (L63/64 for 32B), consistent with late-layer safety features~\\citep{wollschlager2025geometry}; R1-32B's residualized selectivity is only 3.0pp (\\cref{app:control}), below the 5pp threshold of \\citet{hewitt2019control}. Full-range R1-8B analysis confirms all four models exhibit the divergence (\\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}).\\looseness=-1",
  "new_string": "Our five-metric ablation (\\cref{sec:layer-results}) shows crossing rate reverses the central finding via shallow-layer compression (\\cref{app:cosine_volatility}), confirmed label-independent (\\cref{app:random_cp}) and architecture-independent (R1-8B full-layer sweep; \\cref{tab:r18b_full_layer}). This separates the contributions: layer selection bias (Contribution~1) generalizes from representation geometry; the HS advantage (Contribution~2) is sample-dependent. }
```
